# Changelog

All notable changes to this workspace configuration are documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/).

---

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- **[2026-08-23]**: feat(scripts): **intentional-duplicate marker hashes — Stage 1b of ADR-0059, making constitution→template duplication drift-detectable (`verify-adr-governance.ts` 1.0.0 → 1.1.0).** The 9 `<!-- intentional-duplicate: workspace standards §N -->` markers (common context.md §3 Git/PR Workflow; co-abap/co-design/co-develop/co-game/co-security/co-work context.md §8 Coding Guidelines; `variant.context.template.md` ×2) marked sections maintained as local duplicates of constitution sections but carried no hash, so source drift was undetectable. Each marker now carries `source: docs/constitution/<NN>-<slug>.md; hash: <sha256-8>` (whole-file hash; §3 → `03-pr-workflow.md`, §8 → `08-coding-guidelines.md`, resolved generically by section-number prefix). The validator's new second phase recomputes and WARNs on mismatch ("source changed since hash was recorded — review the duplicated section and re-seed"), flags hashless markers, and reports `n/9 in sync`; a new `--update-marker-hashes` mode seeds/refreshes markers in place (mirrors `verify-readme-sync.ts --update-hashes` UX). Compatibility: `lifecycle-sync-audit.ts` Check D (informational duplicate registry) parses the old prefix unchanged — its capture regex already stopped at the marker's first `;`. Design note: the check lives in `verify-adr-governance.ts` itself rather than `verify-readme-sync.ts` (per ADR-0059 §2) — the README pipeline stays README-scoped and one governance validator owns both reflection checks behind the single `--governance-check` audit flag (no audit.ts change in this PR). Boundary: spans root scripts + 8 template marker files — documented exception to the Workspace & Template Boundary Policy (ADR-0057 precedent; same-session PR-A/PR-B pattern from earlier today). Verified: seeded 9/9 in sync; PM independent negative test — tampered hash → exactly one stale WARN (8/9) → restored → 9/9; `audit.ts --governance-check` all-pass; plain audit unchanged all-pass; `validate-templates` 0 errors (2 pre-existing co-deck WARNs); `lifecycle-sync-audit` 0 errors; `docs/constitution/` byte-identical after testing.
- **[2026-08-23]**: feat(scripts): **new `verify-adr-governance.ts` (1.0.0) + ADR-0059 — WARN-first detection for the upward-reflection gap the governance review found (ADR→constitution wiring was automated nowhere; ADR-0057's mechanism landed via #612 with zero constitution pointers, requiring manual #616).** The validator scans `docs/adr/*.md` (top level; parses frontmatter `status:`/`date:` with a `**Status**:` body-line fallback, handling the mixed pre-frontmatter formats and value variants like `"Accepted"`/`Accepted (Amended …)`/`active`), applies an in-file cutoff (2026-08-23 — ADRs dated on/after it require linkage, earlier ones grandfathered to avoid ~50-file backfill noise), and WARNs when an Accepted/active ADR is referenced by neither its canonical `ADR-00NN` form nor its filename anywhere in the governance corpus (CONSTITUTION.md + `docs/constitution/` + `docs/governance/`, 31 files). WARN exits 0; exit 1 only on operational failure. Wired into `audit.ts` (2.19.1→2.20.0) behind `--governance-check`, mirroring the ADR-0055 `--spec-check` Stage-1 burn-in pattern (dev-sync wiring = Stage 2 once clean); L1 `audit.ts` mirror propagated + both `SCRIPTS.md` rows aligned (lifecycle Check B). `docs/constitution/09-operations-workflow.md` documents the new audit flag — and the validator dogfooded itself on first run: ADR-0059 initially WARNed as unlinked and got its constitution pointer (3/3 linked after). ADR-0059 also records the rejected alternatives (frontmatter retrofit + 58-file backfill, constitution last-reviewed footers + 12-month WARN, auto-generated ADR index, auto-writing governance prose, ERROR severity on day one) and stages the intentional-duplicate marker hashes as Stage 1b inside `verify-adr-governance.ts` itself (not a separate validator). PM review corrected two factual errors in the agent-authored ADR draft (marker locations: template context.md files, not variant READMEs; PR #616's actual file list). Verified: fixture round-trip (unlinked ADR-9999 → WARN + exit 0 → deleted → clean), `audit.ts --governance-check` all-pass, plain `audit.ts` unchanged all-pass, `validate-templates` 0 errors (2 pre-existing co-deck WARNs), `lifecycle-sync-audit` clean.
- **[2026-08-23]**: feat(country): **country-scoped env keys — the `country_scoped_assets` registry now governs environment-variable credentials (ADR-0058, closing the country-profile design review's W1 gap).** The registry criterion ("country-specific data-system access, never language") had been modeled only for `skills`/`scripts`, so `templates/common/.env.sample` shipped `DART_API_KEY` (opendart.fss.or.kr — the data system behind `k-dart`) and `LAW_API_OC` (open.law.go.kr — behind `k-law`) to EVERY scaffolded project regardless of `--country`, an asset-universe hole rather than a missed line of code. The registry gains an `"env"` section (`{"DART_API_KEY": "KR", "LAW_API_OC": "KR"}`); `.env.sample` wraps the key block in `# >>> country-scoped:KR` / `# <<< country-scoped:KR` marker comments (the Korean signup comment English-ized — `.env` files are invisible to the Markdown language guard, English-by-convention per the ADR); `prune-country-scoped-assets.ts` (0.1.0 → 0.2.0) deletes every marker block whose code differs from the target country at both scaffold entry points and leaves unbalanced-marker files untouched with a warning (the validator owns that failure); `validate-templates.ts` (1.11.0 → 1.12.0) adds four env-integrity ERRORs (registered key absent from every block, unbalanced/nested markers, block-code/registry disagreement, unregistered key inside a block); new `scripts/verify-country-prune.ts` (1.0.0) fixture-verifies the helper end-to-end in a temp dir across US / none / KR / unbalanced-marker scenarios (4/4 PASS). Governance: ADR-0058 records the decision — alternatives rejected: leave unpruned + document as optional, per-country `.env` fragment files, marker-less line pruning — plus the design review's accepted risks (multi-jurisdiction engagements stay single-primary-jurisdiction YAGNI; agent/skill prose references to profiles are not machine-verified); constitution §6.8 registry example and deployment rule now cover env blocks. Mirror discipline: `templates/common/docs/workspace-schema.json` updated same-commit (ADR-0053); root/common `country_scoped_assets` sections byte-identical. Verified: `verify-country-prune` 4/4, `validate-templates` 0 errors (2 pre-existing co-deck WARNs), `audit.ts` all-pass.
- **[2026-08-23]**: feat(country): **country profile mechanism — country-specific characteristics management for variants, with scaffold-time deployment filtering (PR-A of the A→D sequence; PR-B/C/D migrate co-hr/co-export/co-news onto KR profiles).** Variants can now own country profiles at `docs/countries/<CODE>.md` (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 or well-known region code) declared via the optional `variant.json` `country_config` key (`profiles_dir`/`supported`/`default` — region-neutral `null` stays the required default); profiles are advisory knowledge loaded at Phase 0 intake, never auto-executed. Schema SSOT: `variant_extensions._shared.country_config` + new top-level `country_scoped_assets` registry in `docs/workspace-schema.json` (mirrored to `templates/common/docs/`), plus `country_config` property in `docs/templates/variant.schema.json`. **Deployment filter**: `k-law`/`k-dart`/`k-kosis` (KR-scoped data-system skills) were previously copied into EVERY generated project and L3 draft regardless of target country; the new shared helper `scripts/helpers/prune-country-scoped-assets.ts` prunes scoped assets unless the target country matches — wired into `new-project.ts` (new `--country` flag + interactive prompt defaulting to region-neutral, `Target-Jurisdiction` provenance line, `docs/countries/ACTIVE.md` pointer file, `template-version.txt` `country=`) and `create-l3-scaffold.ts` (new `--country` flag; prune placed at Step 4.5 AFTER the root-skills copy — an earlier placement let Step 4 re-copy `skills/k-*` straight back in; also fixed `country` missing from the `parseArgs` return, which silently made `--country KR` behave region-neutral). Promotion flows (`project-to-variant.ts`, `l3-to-variant-pipeline.ts`) now exclude `ACTIVE.md` from templates, preserve `country_config`, and refuse scoped-skill fork copies. Validation: `validate-templates.ts` 1.10.0→1.11.0 new B-05 country-config check (profile existence, frontmatter `code`/filename match, `last_verified` staleness WARN, undeclared `docs/countries` WARN, jurisdiction-anchored description WARN, registry integrity). Docs: ADR-0057; `templates/common/docs/country-profiles.md` convention doc (ships into generated projects); country ≠ language principle (country_config vs `i18n.locale_codes` are separate axes); `create-variant` (1.3.0→1.4.0) + `project-to-variant` (1.2.0→1.3.0) skill guidance updated in all 4 mirrors; `templates/README.md`/`README_ko.md` + common `SKILLS.md` KR-scope annotations. Verified: region-neutral scaffolds prune k-* from every mirror (workspace-root project + L3 draft), `--country KR` keeps all 9 k-* dirs (3 skills × 3 mirrors), invalid codes rejected with a clear error, `validate-templates` 0 errors, `audit.ts` all-pass.

### Changed
- **[2026-08-23]**: changed(templates): **template PM agent tier high → medium (all 4 platforms), with the tier-layering rule and per-variant escape hatch documented.** `templates/common/agents/pm.md` re-declares `tier: medium` (claude/gemini/antigravity/gemini-cli; `last_updated` → 2026-08-23) — since all 11 variant `pm.md` skeletons declare no tier, the resolve-variants spread-merge propagates this everywhere; the workspace-root PM stays High (workspace governance + design adjudication vs project orchestration). Roster + doctrine aligned: common `AGENTS.md` PM rows (§1 roster + §4.1 subagent roster) High→Medium with an escape-hatch sentence (design-adjudication-heavy work dispatches to the architect or the variant's High-tier design specialist; a variant whose PM must own design adjudication re-declares `tier: high` in its own frontmatter); `context.md` tier-mapping doctrine reworded (High = complex reasoning/planning/architecture, Medium gains orchestration/coordination) + a tier-layering note after the Claude mapping. Straggler sweep findings fixed: `docs/variants/pm-yaml-schema.md` PM dispatch-protocol examples (4 sites) high→medium + layering note under Tier Values; 7 variant `AGENTS.md` mirrors of the stale "Used exclusively by the PM/Architect" High-tier line reworded to "the architect and High-tier design specialists" (variant AGENTS.md files are scaffold-resolved once, never auto-regenerated — co-design/co-develop/co-consult/co-work/co-game/co-deck/co-security). Intentionally untouched: co-game `docs/designs/*` dated execution plans (point-in-time records), non-PM agent tiers (architect.md in co-abap/co-develop/co-game verified still High — the escape-hatch prerequisite), deliverable-type tier examples for non-PM agents. Verified: `validate-templates` 0 errors (2 pre-existing co-deck WARNs), `audit.ts` all-pass (pm.md L0→L1→L2 consistency green), diff = 11 files all under templates/, no variant pm.md skeleton edited.
- **[2026-08-23]**: docs(country): **templates prose pass closing the country-profile design review's W2/W3 gaps and the co-deck research-mandate softening (PR-B; the env-marker half of PR-B landed with #620).** **common `context.md`** — the last KR default in the region-neutral shared template is gone: the language-policy row "Conversational replies to user | Korean (default)" becomes "Match the user's language; when an active country profile defines a communication default (KR: Korean), follow it" (grep-verified: the only remaining `Korean (default)` in templates/ is co-deck's AUTHORING_GUIDELINES output-language table, a deliberate handbook setting, out of scope). **common `country-profiles.md`** — three additions: a multi-jurisdiction sentence (ACTIVE.md records the PRIMARY jurisdiction; engagements spanning more may load extra profiles as secondaries listed in the body — one primary at a time, YAGNI per ADR-0058); a Personal/Global Skill Bundles subsection (user-global `~/.claude/skills` assets are machine-personal, outside workspace governance, invisible to pruning/validators, never assumed present — the review's global k-bundle finding documented, not absorbed); and a Profile Freshness & Ownership section (owner = the shipping variant; cadence = every 6 months or before a release leaning on the profile, the validator's 12-month WARN is a backstop not the process; draft→active on first full verification; active→stale on WARN or known regulatory change; stale stays loadable and must be flagged at Phase 0 intake, never auto-deleted). **co-deck research mandate softened (user-approved)** — "Always search in both Korean and English — single-language coverage is incomplete" absolutism becomes "Korean and English by default; adjust to the engagement's target market and audience when they differ" across `agents/research.md` (3 mandate sites + both `last_updated` stamps → 2026-08-23), `skills/research/SKILL.md` 1.2.0→1.2.1 (description + Step 2, `last_reviewed` 2026-08-23) and its `.claude`/`.gemini`/`.agents` mirrors (cp-synced, diff-verified identical), and `docs/co-deck.context.md` quality rule #1. The Korean font pipeline stays unchanged (font availability is rendering capability, not jurisdiction anchoring); no `country_config` added to co-deck (its Korean-ness is market+language; the 5 mandatory profile sections would be empty). Verified: `validate-templates` 0 errors (2 pre-existing co-deck WARNs), `audit.ts` all-pass, `verify-readme-sync` 13/13, `validate-md-language` clean.
- **[2026-08-23]**: fix(scripts): **closed the body-drift blind spot in `verify-readme-sync.ts` (1.1.1 → 1.2.0) and completed the ADR-0013 removal of inert `PLACEHOLDER` hash fields.** The static audit only compared EN `content_hash` vs KO `translated_from_hash` — never either against the actual README body — so a body edit that skipped the hash refresh left both hashes equally stale and passed silently. v1.2.0 adds a drift gate to `runStaticAudit`: when a pair matches and tracking is active, the stored EN hash is recomputed against the current body and a mismatch FAILs with both hashes and the fix command; the `PLACEHOLDER` sentinel and absent-field cases stay PASS ("tracking not active", ADR-0013). Since pre-commit always runs the static audit when READMEs are staged, body drift is now commit-blocked. **The gate caught 2 real defects on first run**: co-develop (`0057c576…` → `2aa680da…`) and co-game (`907baf6d…` → `a8f2cf58…`) carried hashes written from a stale body state by #617's resync; both EN hashes refreshed via the maintainer path and KO `translated_from_hash` mirrored — live proof the check works, no synthetic negative test needed. **ADR-0013 completion**: removed the dead sentinel fields from the 4 root index READMEs (`README.md` `content_hash`; `README_ko/es/ja.md` `translated_from_hash`), `templates/common/README_ko.md`, and the two scaffold templates `templates/common/docs/README.template.md` / `README_ko.template.md` (which stamped `PLACEHOLDER` into every newly generated variant README) — root/common now uniformly report "tracking not active" (deliberate: activating root hash tracking would hard-fail the WS-12 translation-lag allowance on `README_es/ja`), and new variants start untracked until a maintainer activates tracking via `--update-hashes`. `sync_version` retained everywhere; `Projects/*` copies are separate repositories, out of scope. L0+L1 sync: script published to `templates/common/scripts/`, `scripts/SCRIPTS.md` + L1 mirror registry bumped to 1.2.0. Gates: `verify-readme-sync` 13/13 PASS, `validate-templates` 0 errors (2 pre-existing co-deck WARNs), `audit.ts` all-pass, `lifecycle-sync-audit` clean.
- **[2026-08-23]**: docs(constitution): **wired the country-profile mechanism into the governance documents (PR-E, closing out the A→D migration's documentation debt).** The PR-A mechanism (#612) landed with ADR-0057 + the convention doc but without pointers from the constitution/contract layer, so an agent reading only the governance docs would never discover it. Five surgical additions: **(1)** constitution §7.3.5 *Target Country Selection* — the scaffold-time `--country` flow (supported-code validation, `Target-Jurisdiction` provenance line, `template-version.txt` `country=` row, `docs/countries/ACTIVE.md` pointer), region-neutral as the `--yes`/CI default, and the post-overlay pruning of `country_scoped_assets` skills from all 4 mirror directories via `prune-country-scoped-assets.ts` (same helper in `create-l2-scaffold.ts`); **(2)** constitution §4.3 *Country ≠ Language* — `i18n.locale_codes` and `country_config` are independent axes (CH → de/fr/it; `zh-CN`/`zh-TW` are language+region hybrids, never country codes; a profile's Language section references locale_codes, never redefines it; language skills must never be country-scoped); **(3)** constitution §6.8 *Country-Scoped Skills* — registry-only design (no SKILL.md frontmatter field), the deployment/pruning rule, the agents-reference-scoped-skills-only-via-country-profile rule (no `required_skills` — pruning would strand dangling refs), and the validator's registry-integrity + dangling-ref checks; **(4)** `docs/governance/variant-contract.md` gains the *Country Profile Standard* section (country_config shape, `default: null` policy, 5-section profile format, Check 2 `country-config`/`country-scoped-assets` enforcement, backtick language convention) and a `docs/countries/<CODE>.md` row in the Optional Files table (currently co-hr/co-export/co-news/co-consult); **(5)** `templates/common/docs/context.md` gains a *Country Profiles* pointer subsection under Documentation Standards so every scaffolded project's immutable context references `ACTIVE.md` and the convention doc. Verified: `validate-templates` 0 errors (2 pre-existing co-deck WARNs), `audit.ts` all-pass (a double-backticked Korean statute example in variant-contract.md tripped the language gate on first run — replaced with a prose description, the same double-backtick trap PR-D hit in KR.md).
- **[2026-08-23]**: feat(co-news): **migrated co-news onto the country-profile mechanism and completed the 8-variant anchor audit (PR-D, closing the A→D sequence).** `variant.json` gains `country_config` (KR) and a de-anchored description — "Korean default, other languages supported" became "output language follows project i18n settings; the KR profile defaults to ko" (reworded from "Korean" to clear the validator's jurisdiction-anchor heuristic). New `docs/countries/KR.md` profile: DART/FSS disclosure framework (receipt-number citation unit), Korean Commercial Act, Act on Press Arbitration and Remedies (`언론중재법`) corrective-report mechanics, Korea Communications Standards Commission, KRW/jo-eok-man formats, k-dart + k-law tooling map with the scoped-deployment rule. 44 replacements across 17 files (38 scripted + 6 stragglers): the recurring description string updated in variant.json + README/README_ko taglines ×3 spots each; pm/context/guides language defaults now "per the active country profile (KR default: ko)" confirmed at Phase 0; DART references conditioned "(KR profile)" / "(KR: DART corp_code)"; legal-researcher "under Korean law" → "under applicable law"; financial-analyst role cells (full + truncated forms) carry "(KR country profile)". Language-conditional content (Korean numeral groupings "when the article is in Korean", style-guide and style-examples source-material zones) intentionally UNCHANGED — country ≠ language. **Audit of the other 8 variants**: co-abap/co-deck/co-design/co-develop/co-game/co-security/co-work clean (only language-name links and ko zones); co-consult's `k-law` fork copy triaged as IN USE (registered in variant.json skill_manifest + AGENTS.md row) → co-consult granted `country_config` + its own lean `docs/countries/KR.md` legitimizing the fork; co-consult's remaining Korea references (financial-statement-analysis DART pipeline, company-intelligence, sample-driven-report-writing) recorded as a follow-up task per the audit policy. Verified: `validate-templates` 0 errors (B-05 green on co-news AND co-consult), `audit.ts` all-pass (KR profiles use the backtick convention for Korean terms).
- **[2026-08-23]**: feat(co-export): **migrated co-export onto the country-profile mechanism (PR-C of the A→D sequence) — the variant is now region-neutral by default with Korean specifics owned by `docs/countries/KR.md`.** `variant.json` gains `country_config` (supported: KR, region-neutral default; the description was already region-neutral). New `docs/countries/KR.md` profile: customs/trade-statute table (Customs Act, Foreign Trade Act + Strategic Items Trade Control Notice, sanctions, drawback, trade-insurance laws), KR FTA network (RCEP, KORUS, Korea–EU, Korea–China, +12 more), KCS/KOSTI/MOTIE/K-SURE/KITA/UNI-PASS authorities and systems, customs-broker licensing note, KRW/Asia-Seoul formats, k-law/k-kosis tooling map. 35 files de-anchored across 4 lifecycle layers: 8 agents + 1 lifecycle governance record now speak of "the home jurisdiction (see the active country profile under docs/countries/)" with Korea-specifics kept only as explicit "(KR: …)" markers (KOSTI, Korea Customs Service, CVCI, KOTRA, HSK tariff lines, UNI-PASS); the variant's structural "Korea-side vs destination-side" labeling is now "home-jurisdiction-based vs destination-country-based" (agents, context.md Regulatory Scope + Domain Rules, both user guides EN/KO); 6 skills edited in 4-mirror lockstep (skills/, .claude/, .gemini/, .agents/) — 22 skill files in all. Language-policy notes: agent files' Korean-statute references follow the backtick convention; the KR profile wraps Korean proper nouns in backticks so the audit language gate passes without a lang: ko declaration (same pattern as co-hr's KR profile). READMEs needed no change (taglines were already region-neutral). Verified: `validate-templates` 0 errors (B-05 country-config green on co-export), `audit.ts` all-pass, residual-anchor grep clean outside KR.md and deliberate "(KR: …)" markers.
- **[2026-08-23]**: feat(co-hr): **migrated co-hr onto the country-profile mechanism (PR-B of the A→D sequence) — the variant is now region-neutral by default with Korean specifics owned by `docs/countries/KR.md`.** `variant.json` gains `country_config` (supported: KR, region-neutral default) and a de-anchored description ("labor-law compliance for the target jurisdiction (country profiles under docs/countries/)…"); new `docs/countries/KR.md` profile carries the 5 normative sections (labor-statute table — Labor Standards Act, Occupational Safety and Health Act, Serious Accidents Punishment Act, etc. — MOEL/Labor Relations Commission/KOSHA authorities, licensed labor-professional note, KRW/Asia-Seoul/business-registration-number formats, k-law/k-kosis tooling map). All 22 touched files de-anchored: 6 agents (labor-compliance-analyst, labor-relations-specialist, safety-health-officer, org-design-consultant, pm + 3 lifecycle governance records) now defer to "the active country profile under docs/countries/ (if none active, confirm the jurisdiction at Phase 0 intake)" with `required_skills` emptied of k-law; the Korean-labor-relations consulting phrase → "labor-relations consulting"; the Korean voluntary-retirement parentheticals dropped from org-design-framework (3 skill mirrors) and the AGENTS.md role cell; context.md Domain Rules now defer statute enumeration to the active profile; both user guides (EN/KO 1:1) condition k-law/k-kosis on "(KR profile)" per WS-11; consulting-report-writing's backtick rule generalized to "the active jurisdiction". Anchors now exist ONLY inside KR.md (grep-verified). README content_hash/translated_from_hash refreshed (2a95ee51…); `verify-readme-sync` all-pass, `validate-templates` 0 errors (B-05 country-config check green on the new country_config), `audit.ts` all-pass. Note: `--update-hashes` surfaced pre-existing hash drift in root/common/co-consult/co-develop/co-game/co-news READMEs (root still `PLACEHOLDER`) — reverted to keep this PR inside the §9 co-hr boundary; follow-up logged for a dedicated hash-resync PR.
- **[2026-08-23]**: docs(readme): brought all 6 variant-index READMEs current with the 8th-variant reality — every enumeration had drifted behind `VERSION_REGISTRY.json` (the SSOT). **co-hr** was missing from all of them: added to the root `README.md` / `README_ko.md` (directory tree, workflow bullet, variant table × each language) and `templates/README.md` / `templates/README_ko.md` (tree + Available Variants table, Last-Updated stamps). Two translations had silently fallen further behind and were backfilled: `README_es.md` / `README_ja.md` were missing co-export + co-news + co-abap entirely (trees, bullets, tables — all three sections still ended at co-game) — all four newer variants added in-language; also corrected `README_ja.md`'s co-game status (marked 🔶 Beta in tree+table although the variant has been Stable everywhere else since before the JA translation's last update). `templates/README_ko.md` was likewise missing the same four variants and backfilled. `scripts/README.md` needs no manual edit (auto-generated by dev-sync step 2.5); variant READMEs themselves are current (co-hr's was generated in #606; its ~80-char role-description truncation and empty skill-description lines match the generator standard across siblings like co-news — a generator limitation, not variant drift).

### Fixed
- **[2026-08-23]**: feat(co-consult): **completed the co-consult de-anchor recorded as the PR-D audit follow-up — 20 files, 89 replacements, closing the A→D country-profile migration's last open item.** `financial-statement-analysis` (4 mirrors): frontmatter description and Context de-anchored ("Korean financial statement analysis pipeline" → home-jurisdiction disclosure system, KR profile: DART) plus a new **Jurisdiction scope** note — under a non-KR active profile or region-neutral engagement agents must not run the DART pipeline and instead request client-supplied disclosures with verification limits flagged (same treatment the KR profile gives the k-law fork). `company-intelligence` (4 mirrors): the "Korean vs foreign" intake binary and decision-tree branch → "home-market vs foreign" with query-language rules kept as language-conditional content ("use the home-market language …; KR profile: Korean" — country ≠ language); source assumptions conditioned "(KR: DART)", "(KR profile: DART/KRX)", credit-rating agencies "(KR: NICE, Korea Ratings, Korea Investors Service)", currency "(KR profile: KRW)", "local finance portals (KR profile: Naver Finance)". `sample-driven-report-writing` (4 mirrors): "DART fallback" → "Disclosure fallback (… no KR-profile DART filing)". Registry hygiene: AGENTS.md K-DART/K-Law rows and `skills/SKILLS.md` links annotated — k-dart is a KR-scoped COMMON skill (deployed only to `--country KR` projects by the `country_scoped_assets` pruning; the co-consult SKILLS.md/AGENTS.md rows reference it unconditionally, dangling in region-neutral scaffolds) while k-law is the variant fork (KR profile only). `docs/co-consult.context.md` (2.4→2.5): "Deliverables are written in **Korean** by default" → deliverable language follows the active country profile (KR default: Korean), matching the co-news wording; Domain Rule 7 and the naming-convention table header likewise. Both user guides (EN/KO 1:1): scenario-table k-dart rows conditioned "(KR profile)" in EN (localized marker in KO) per the co-hr convention, the pipeline walkthrough intro de-anchored, Step 1 k-dart usage conditioned. README/README_ko skill row de-anchored; hashes refreshed (EN `a2151e62…`, KO mirrored). Intentionally unchanged: hwp-document-processing (Korean office FORMAT, not a jurisdiction anchor), terms-ko.json SSOT linkage and Korean-account-name normalization details (language/data conventions of the KR pipeline), k-law SKILL.md's own text (KR-scoped skill describing itself), output-template bilingual-naming placeholders. Verified: `validate-templates` 0 errors (2 pre-existing co-deck WARNs), `audit.ts` all-pass, `verify-readme-sync` all-pass, residual grep clean outside language-conditional text and deliberate "(KR …)" markers.
- **[2026-08-23]**: docs(readme): **resynced stale `content_hash` tracking in 4 variant README pairs (co-consult, co-develop, co-game, co-news) — EN `content_hash` recomputed from the current body and KO `translated_from_hash` mirrored to match (8 files).** The drift surfaced when PR-B's hash refresh rewrote all 13 READMEs and the non-target rewrites were reverted to respect the §9 boundary: those 4 variants' hashes predated recent body edits, so the pair stayed internally consistent while both lying about the content. `verify-readme-sync` cannot catch this class (it only compares EN vs KO frontmatter, never against the actual body) — recomputation via `--update-hashes` was the diagnostic. Root `README.md` and `templates/common/README.md` were deliberately left on the ADR-0013 sentinel (`content_hash`/`translated_from_hash: PLACEHOLDER`, common EN absent entirely): activating real hash tracking on the root index would hard-fail the translation lag that WS-12 explicitly permits (`README_es.md`/`README_ja.md` may trail primary-language updates). The sentinel pair is inert and self-consistent; removing the dead root fields remains optional ADR-0013 completion, not drift. Gates: `verify-readme-sync` all-pass, `validate-templates` 0 errors, `audit.ts` all-pass.

- **[2026-08-21]**: docs(governance): documentation-sync pass over this session's changes — found and fixed 3 gaps where docs had drifted from (or never captured) what the code now does. **(1)** `docs/constitution/08-coding-guidelines.md` §8.10 still asserted the `nul` artifacts were "produced by an external tool" — an hypothesis #601's root-cause finding had falsified (the producer was a `bun build` output path resolving to `nul` *inside* `templates/co-deck/`, hidden by `.gitignore`, propagated by `copyDir`). Rewritten with the true root cause and the layered defense (scaffold-time purge + tracked-tree sweep), plus the non-obvious lesson: a build tool's *output path* can produce the artifact even with zero `> nul` redirects in source. The matching stale comment in `audit.ts` (2.19.0 → 2.19.1, "codegraph, antivirus" suspect list) updated likewise. **(2)** The variant-skill `l2_propagate: false` trap — the self-contradiction that silently dropped co-abap's 12 SAP skills from every scaffold (#598) — was nowhere documented; added a ⚠️ warning to `docs/constitution/06-skill-lifecycle.md` §`l2_propagate` ("never set it on a skill living inside a variant template; a variant skill needs only `scope: <variant-name>`"). **(3)** Verified present, no change needed: WS-10 standard (`variant-contract.md`), the `metadata.type` taxonomy (`06-skill-lifecycle.md`), the extended `extends:` schema rows (`extends-pattern.md`), WS-09's graduation (`variant-contract.md` + `lifecycle-governance.json`), and CONSTITUTION.md §6.6's validator-enforcement claim — now true since #595 wired the framework.

### Fixed
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(scripts): **root-caused the recurring `nul` artifact** — the producer was never an external tool after all, but a 24 KB `nul` file sitting in `templates/co-deck/` itself (a bundled `apply-handbook-theme.ts`, created 2026-08-17 during handbook work, likely by a `bun build` whose output path resolved to `nul` on Windows). Because the template's own `.gitignore` lists `NUL`, the file never appeared in `git status` — invisible to every review — yet `new-project.ts`'s `copyDir` ignores `.gitignore` and **shipped it into every co-deck scaffold**, which is why co-deck projects kept getting an undeletable `nul` while co-news projects did not. This also explains why the earlier 10-minute idle poll across 5 scaffolds observed nothing: the artifact is born at scaffold time (not time-delayed), and the post-scaffold audit's then-existing one-level sweep deleted the project's `nul` before the poll ever checked. Removed the source file, and hardened both layers: **(1)** `new-project.ts` (1.6.0 → 1.7.0) now purges Windows device-name files from the project directory immediately after the variant overlay copy, at the propagation point — verified by planting `templates/co-deck/nul` and confirming the scaffold logs `🗑️ Purged device-name artifact` and comes out clean. **(2)** `audit.ts` (2.18.0 → 2.19.0) — the recursive sweep now covers **tracked top-level directories too** (previously untracked only, so `templates/` — the propagation vector — was never swept), and its success detection was fixed: it re-checked `fs.existsSync()` after `rm`, but Win32 device-name resolution makes that call return `true` for paths that no longer exist (and `false` for backslash forms that do), producing false "could not be auto-deleted" warnings while the file was in fact gone — observed live. The sweep now passes forward-slash paths to Git Bash's `rm` and trusts only the exit status. The user-visible `co-deck/nul` that had been blocking PowerShell `Remove-Item` was swept during verification.

### Changed
- **[2026-08-21]**: feat(skills): established the `metadata.type` taxonomy — the last item left open by the validator track (#595 had dropped the 4-value enum in favor of a shape check, noting 16 organically-grown values with "no documented taxonomy to migrate toward"). Authored that taxonomy in `docs/constitution/06-skill-lifecycle.md` § "Skill metadata.type Taxonomy": a 20-row table (19 observed values + `testing`, reserved) with a meaning and representative skills per value, based on a fresh survey of every `SKILL.md` in the tree. The survey showed the spread is less random than it looked — co-abap deliberately splits `module` (SAP-module reference) from `core` (mandatory workflow), and co-consult uses `domain` for the same concept co-abap calls `module`, so wholesale normalization would erase real distinctions; instead the table documents the distinctions and marks `task` as discouraged (alias of `process`) and `utility` as example-files-only. Decision rule included: reuse the closest value, extend doc + validator list together, no new per-variant subkinds. Enforcement is a new **WARN-level** vocabulary check in `schema-validator.ts` (1.2.0 → 1.3.0) — undocumented values are visible drift, not blocked commits — with the validator's `DOCUMENTED_METADATA_TYPES` list explicitly coupled to the doc ("update together, never one without the other"). All 19 observed values are documented, so the check starts at 0 warnings; verified by re-running the variant registry probe (0 errors, 0 warnings).

### Fixed
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(validate-templates): C-SK-02's `extends`-pattern field check produced nonsense warnings for every variant using the full ADR-0034 override mechanism (`validate-templates.ts` 1.8.0 → 1.9.0). Two stacked defects: **(1)** the check reused the line-based `parseFrontmatter()` helper, which trims each candidate key *before* testing for indentation — so indented prose lines inside `variant_overrides:` block scalars whose text happened to contain a colon were reported as top-level frontmatter keys (warning text literally listed `<!-- VARIANT-SECTION`, `1. HS classification memorandum`, … as "unexpected fields"). Now parsed with real YAML (`js-yaml`), giving true top-level keys. **(2)** the allowlist omitted `remove_sections` and `variant_overrides` — the ADR-0039/ADR-0034 override mechanism itself, consumed at scaffold time by `merge-frontmatter.ts` — plus `status` (documented optional at all levels), and `owner`/`capabilities` (pass-through metadata; `capability-validator.ts` reads pm's capabilities, which #597 just registered). All five added to the allowlist and documented in `docs/architecture/extends-pattern.md`'s schema table, which previously had no rows for them despite the code path dating to ADR-0034. **Also graduated WS-09** (`docs/<variant>.context.md` structure conformance) from `warningOnly` to a hard FAIL — the graduation its severity policy prescribed "once all non-exempt variants conform": `co-news`, the last holdout, was brought into conformance by #598. Mirrors WS-08's 2026-08-09 graduation. `validate-templates.ts` warnings now stand at 2, both the scheduled `co-deck` `measure`-skill deprecation notice (`removal-date: 2026-09-08`) functioning as designed.

### Fixed
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(templates): cleared 6 of the 8 remaining `validate-templates.ts` warnings — and the WS-06 batch turned out to be **a silent scaffold bug, not a labeling nit**. All 13 domain skills flagged by WS-06 carried `l2_propagate: false` alongside a variant `scope:`, which is self-contradictory inside a variant template (a variant skill exists precisely to propagate into projects scaffolded from that variant) — and `new-project.ts`'s safety-net honors the flag, so **every `co-abap` project has been scaffolded without its 12 SAP skills, and every `co-consult` project without `financial-statement-analysis`**. Verified empirically before and after: a fresh co-abap scaffold contained 0 of the 12 SAP skills before the fix, all 12 after. Removed the flag from all 4 platform copies (`skills/`, `.claude/`, `.gemini/`, `.agents/`) of the 13 skills, and corrected their `scope:` values to the variant-name convention (`scope: variant` → `scope: co-abap`; `scope: common` → `scope: co-consult`). Also: grafted the `SessionStart` git-hooks hook (present in every other variant) into `co-abap`'s `.claude/` and `.gemini/` `settings.json`, clearing 2 shared-key warnings; and brought `co-news`' `context.md` into WS-09 structural conformance by adding the 4 missing standard slots (`Tech Stack`, `Development Workflow`, `File Organization Policy`, `Domain Rules`), each with newsroom-specific content derived from the variant's own conventions (the `deliverables/drafts/<article>/` layout from the reporter/visual-editor specs, the two hard gates, the language-split rule) rather than generic filler — WS-09 now passes for co-news. The 2 remaining warnings are deliberate: `co-deck`'s `measure` skill is a scheduled deprecation (`removal-date: 2026-09-08`, superseded by `prep-pdf`) whose warning is functioning as designed, and the C-SK-02 pair is a root-side validator defect fixed in the companion PR.
