### Added
- **[2026-08-21]**: feat(audit): wired the dormant `scripts/validators/` framework into `audit.ts` (`audit.ts` 2.17.0 → 2.18.0) — a new "Variant registry validation" check runs `runAllValidators()` across all `templates/co-*` variants on every audit. `CONSTITUTION.md` §6.6 has *documented* this enforcement since the framework was written, but nothing imported it: `scripts/validators/` was dead code, which is exactly how `phase: active`/`beta` drift, 6 unparseable `SKILL.md` frontmatters, and `co-abap`'s missing `role`/`version` fields all went unnoticed (each found and fixed this session only because the framework was measured manually as prep for this wiring). The wiring is L0-only — `scripts/validators/` is layer L0 and not propagated to `templates/common/scripts/`, so the check guards on directory existence and the L1 copy skips it cleanly. Error-severity findings Fail the audit; the 14 current warnings (8 `unknown-capability`, 6 `orphan-skill-reference`) surface in the PASS message so the drift stays visible rather than being defined away. Pre-work in the same PR: `schema-validator.ts` (1.1.0 → 1.2.0) aligned to measured reality — `last_reviewed` no longer required (an L0-only convention absent from 92/100 agents; its date-pattern check still applies when present), `beta` added to `VALID_LIFECYCLE_PHASES` (used consistently by exactly the two `beta`-status variants — the enum was stale, not the data), `extends:` stubs skipped (validating pm.md standalone reported the entire required set as missing — 46 of the original 160 errors), and the 4-value `metadata.type` enum dropped in favor of a non-empty-string shape check (16 organically-grown values in use with no documented taxonomy to migrate toward); `variant-type-registry.ts` (1.0.0 → 1.1.0) registered `abap-development`, which `co-abap`'s `variant.json` has declared since its migration. Blast radius across the alignment: 160 errors → 0.

### Fixed
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(templates): repaired 6 `SKILL.md` files whose YAML frontmatter **did not parse** — found by wiring up the dormant `scripts/validators/` framework, whose `parseFrontmatter()` silently returns `{}` on YAML errors, meaning these skills' metadata (name, version, triggers) has been unreadable to any consumer that parses frontmatter. **(1)** `co-abap`'s `post-write-chain` skill, all 4 platform copies (`skills/`, `.claude/`, `.gemini/`, `.agents/`): the unquoted `description:` value contains `gate: SyntaxCheck`, and `gate:` parses as a nested mapping key — YAML error `bad indentation of a mapping entry (2:131)`. Fixed by single-quoting the whole value. **(2)** `templates/common/skills/k-dart` and `k-law`: Korean trigger entries written as a backtick-wrapped plain list item fail because a backtick is a YAML reserved indicator and cannot start a plain scalar. Fixed by double-quoting the value while *retaining the backticks inside the quotes*, so each trigger parses to its originally-authored literal value AND the markdown language checker (`validate-md-language.ts`) continues to treat them as inline code spans (it strips backticked text before Korean detection; removing the backticks had flagged these files as language violations). Full-tree sweep after the fix: 677 `SKILL.md` files scanned, 0 unparseable.

### Fixed
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(templates): added the missing `role:` and `version:` frontmatter fields to `co-abap`'s 19 agents. Both are required by `schemas/agent.schema.json`, and `co-abap` was the sole variant missing them — the other 81 agents across 9 variants have both — so this is variant drift rather than a schema that overreaches. Found while measuring the blast radius of wiring up the dormant `scripts/validators/` framework (160 errors, of which these were 38). Each `role` is read back from the governance record authored earlier today rather than independently reworded, so the runtime definition and its governance record state the same role; `version` is `1.0.0`, matching `variant.json`. Prerequisite for the validator wiring, which would otherwise fail against these 19.

### Added
- **[2026-08-21]**: feat(audit): `'> nul' redirect check` (`audit.ts` 2.16.0 → 2.17.0) — closes **Layer 4** of the 2026-08-07 `nul` meeting (`memory/archive/meeting-2026-08-07-prevent-nul-file-creation.md`), whose action item had sat unchecked for two weeks. Where the recursive sweep is remediation, this is prevention: the audit now fails on any `> nul` / `2> nul` occurrence in `.ts`, `.js`, `.mjs`, `.sh`, `.ps1`, `.cmd`, `.bat`, or `.githooks/` files. `.md` is deliberately excluded — the governance docs must quote the banned pattern in order to ban it — and comment lines are stripped before matching for the same reason (`audit.ts`'s own comments discuss `> nul` at length). A `nul-lint-ignore` line marker provides an escape hatch, currently used only by this check's own two diagnostic strings, which necessarily quote what they forbid. Starts clean at 422 files scanned, confirming the earlier finding that no workspace source produces these artifacts. Verified in both directions: a planted `echo hi > nul 2>&1` in a `.sh` file is caught with file:line, and removing it returns the check to PASS.

### Changed
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(audit): the Windows device-name sweep added earlier today is now **recursive** rather than one level deep (`audit.ts` 2.16.0 → 2.17.0), completing **Layer 3** of the same 2026-08-07 meeting, which specified "recursive" from the start. Depth is not cosmetic here: a device-name file at *any* depth blocks deleting every parent directory above it, so a one-level sweep left the identical symptom one directory down. Skips `node_modules`, `.git`, `.venv`, `.bun`, `dist`, `build`, `.next`, `coverage`, and guards against pathological trees with a depth cap of 8. Verified by planting `aux` at depth 4 (`zz-depth/a/b/c/aux`) and `prn` at depth 2 — the previous one-level implementation would have caught neither; both are now auto-deleted. **Layer 2** of that meeting (`nul`/`NUL` in every `.gitignore`) was found already complete — present in the root and all 11 template `.gitignore` files — its checkbox simply never ticked.

- **[2026-08-21]**: feat(validate-templates): WS-10 tightened now that every variant conforms (`validate-templates.ts` 1.7.0 → 1.8.0). `WS10_EXEMPT_VARIANTS` is emptied — `co-abap`'s 19 agents are now checked like everyone else's — and the `lifecycle.governance` pointer-resolution sub-assertion is promoted from `warn()` to the policy-aware `report()`, so an unresolvable governance pointer can no longer land. The exempt set is deliberately kept rather than deleted: a newly promoted variant may legitimately need a grace period before its governance records exist. Verified: WS-10 now reports `co-abap agent lifecycle frontmatter OK (19 agent(s) checked)` with 0 errors across all variants.

### Fixed
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(audit): `audit.ts` (2.15.0 → 2.16.0) now sweeps Windows reserved device-name artifacts (`nul`, `aux`, `con`, `prn`, `com1-9`, `lpt1-9`) **one level into untracked local project directories**, not just the workspace root. The existing sweep only ever saw `./nul`; in practice these land inside scaffolded project directories — observed 2026-08-21 in two of six freshly scaffolded projects — where nothing cleaned them. The user-visible symptom is that the entire project directory then becomes undeletable from PowerShell, because `Remove-Item` resolves `nul` to the Win32 device rather than to the file (Git Bash's `rm -f -- <path>` does delete it, and is what the sweep shells out to). Investigation found **no repo script writes `> nul`** — a full-tree scan returns only the prohibition rules in `CLAUDE.md`/`GEMINI.md`/`docs/constitution/08-coding-guidelines.md` — so the producer is an external tool and could not be fixed at the source; the artifact also could not be reproduced synthetically (Windows maps the name to the device even through the `\\?\` extended-length prefix) nor by 10 minutes of idle polling across five scaffolds. Sweeping makes recurrence self-healing regardless of producer. Emits a WARN per artifact (with the exact Git Bash removal command if auto-deletion fails) rather than failing the audit. Verified by planting `aux`/`com9`/`lpt9`/`prn` — device names that *are* creatable, exercising the identical code path — inside a scaffolded project and confirming all four were auto-deleted.
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(templates): `co-abap` now scaffolds a clean project — it was the last variant still producing a failing post-scaffold audit. Three separate defects, all pre-existing: **(1)** no `docs/lifecycle/agents/` directory existed at all, so all 19 non-pm agents had nowhere to point — authored 19 governance records (`## Overview` / `## Phase History` / `## Acceptance Criteria`), with each **Role** derived from the leading phrase of the agent's own `description:` field rather than invented (e.g. `fi-analyst` → "FI Module Analyst", `gui-scripter` → "SAP GUI Scripting & Automation Specialist (LAST RESORT)"), and `created` taken from `variant.json` `lifecycle.statusSince` (2026-08-15, its migration date). **(2)** Added the matching `lifecycle:` frontmatter block to the same 19 agents, completing the backfill deferred from the 59-agent pass earlier this session. **(3)** Surfaced only once (1) and (2) were fixed and `validate-agents.ts` stopped short-circuiting: all 19 agents declared `tier:` as a bare scalar (`tier: medium`) rather than the per-platform map every other variant uses, so `agent-lifecycle-audit.ts` reported 57 errors (3 required platforms × 19 agents). Converted to the standard `tier: {claude, gemini, antigravity, gemini-cli}` shape, replicating each agent's existing value across all four platforms — the 1 `high` / 4 `low` / 14 `medium` distribution is preserved exactly. Verified end-to-end: a fresh `co-abap` project now reports `[PASS] Agent audit: all agents healthy`, `[PASS] Agent validation: all agents valid`, and `✅ All checks passed`. **All 10 variants now scaffold green.**
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(templates): corrected `co-security`'s 5 agent lifecycle blocks (`patch-engineer`, `pentester`, `red-team-lead`, `report-writer`, `threat-modeler`). Each set `governance: lifecycle-manager` — an agent *name* where a `docs/lifecycle/agents/*.md` path belongs — which satisfied `validate-agents.ts` (it only checks the key is non-empty) while resolving to nothing. Found by the new WS-10 check on its first run. The governance records existed correctly the whole time, so only the pointers needed fixing. Also reconciled `phase: active` → `production` to match each record's `- **Phase**:` line; `active` was drift unique to this variant and is not among the phase values any other variant uses. This clears the last WS-10 warnings, unblocking promotion of WS-10's pointer-resolution sub-assertion from WARN to FAIL.

### Added
- **[2026-08-21]**: feat(validate-templates): new Check WS-10 — variant `agents/*.md` must carry a `lifecycle:` frontmatter block with `phase` + `governance` (`validate-templates.ts` 1.6.0 → 1.7.0). Closes the enforcement asymmetry that caused the 59-agent backfill in the same session: `validate-agents.ts` has always required these fields of an L2 *project* (and runs inside `audit.ts`), but nothing validated the L1 *template* the project is scaffolded from, so the requirement drifted unnoticed across 8 of 10 variants. WS-10 now demands of the template exactly what `validate-agents.ts` demands of its output. Skips `README.md`/`README_ko.md` and any file with `extends:` in frontmatter (i.e. `pm.md`, an L1-B stub inheriting lifecycle from the L0 root agent — requiring a literal block would duplicate the SSOT). `co-abap` is exempted via `WS10_EXEMPT_VARIANTS` (same precedent as WS-09): it has no `docs/lifecycle/agents/` directory, so its 19 agents need governance records *authored*, which is a content task rather than a mechanical backfill. Unlike WS-08/WS-09 this ships as a hard **FAIL** immediately rather than starting `warningOnly`, since every non-exempt variant already conforms. One sub-assertion stays WARN-only by design — whether `lifecycle.governance` resolves to a real file — because that is stricter than `validate-agents.ts` itself (which only checks the key is non-empty), so failing on it would block commits over a defect that doesn't break a scaffolded project's audit. It caught a real one on its first run: `co-security`'s 5 agents all set `governance: lifecycle-manager`, an agent *name* rather than a `docs/lifecycle/agents/*.md` path — flagged for a follow-up templates fix, after which the sub-assertion can be promoted to `fail()`. Verified by deleting a `lifecycle:` block from `co-deck/agents/design.md` and confirming WS-10 fails, then restoring. Documented in `docs/governance/variant-contract.md` alongside the WS-08/WS-09 standards.

### Fixed
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(scripts): `new-project.ts` (1.5.4 → 1.6.0) now **regenerates** `agents/pm.md`'s `lifecycle:` block instead of deleting it. §2.5 strips L1-B metadata from the resolved pm.md, and `delete fm.lifecycle` was sound in intent — L0's block carries `created`/`last_updated` dates describing the *workspace's* history, which are meaningless in a fresh project — but it left every scaffolded project failing its own post-scaffold audit, since `validate-agents.ts` requires `lifecycle.phase` + `lifecycle.governance` in every `agents/*.md`. This made `pm.md` a guaranteed error in **all 10 variants**, including `co-export` and `co-security` whose other agents were already compliant, and it was the sole remaining error after the 59-agent template backfill. Now rewrites the block with `phase` carried over from the source (defaulting to `production`), `created`/`last_updated` set to the scaffold date, and `governance: docs/lifecycle/agents/pm.md` — a path that already resolves in the scaffolded tree. `projectDate` was hoisted from §5.5b to §2.5 since both now need it. Verified end-to-end: fresh `co-deck` and `co-work` projects now scaffold with `[PASS] Agent validation: all agents valid` and a fully green audit (was 13 errors for `co-deck` at the start of the session). `co-abap` still reports 19 — its known, separately-tracked gap, unchanged by this fix.
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(templates): backfilled the `lifecycle:` frontmatter block into 59 variant agent runtime definitions across 7 variants (`co-consult` 10, `co-deck` 12, `co-design` 7, `co-develop` 6, `co-game` 12, `co-news` 6, `co-work` 6). `validate-agents.ts` requires `lifecycle.phase` + `lifecycle.governance` in every `agents/*.md`, but only `co-export` and `co-security` ever carried it — so `bun scripts/new-project.ts <name> --variant <v>` produced a project that **failed its own post-scaffold audit** for 8 of 10 variants (reported live for `co-deck`: 13 validation errors on a freshly scaffolded project). The governance records already existed and already passed Part 2 of the validator, so only the runtime pointer was missing; several records even claimed "Verified governance record and lifecycle frontmatter" in their Phase History while the frontmatter had never been added. Each block was derived from the agent's own governance record rather than hand-written — `phase` from `- **Phase**:`, `created` from the first dated `## Phase History` entry, `last_updated` from the agent's existing `last_updated:` field — so `co-news` correctly lands on `phase: beta` while the other six get `production`. Purely additive (59 files, +295 lines, no existing line modified). Verified by scaffolding a throwaway project from `co-deck`: 13 errors → 1. `co-abap` is deliberately **not** included — it has no `docs/lifecycle/agents/` directory at all, so its 19 agents need governance records authored first (tracked as follow-up, not a mechanical backfill). The remaining 1 error is `agents/pm.md`, which fails in all 10 variants for an unrelated root-side reason and is fixed separately.

### Added
- **[2026-08-21]**: docs(readme): added the "Claude Code and Multi-Agent Harness" introductory course (https://5throck.github.io/intro-to-ai-harness/) to the Learning Resources section of all 4 README language variants (`README.md`, `README_ko.md`, `README_ja.md`, `README_es.md`), each with a translated description matching that file's language, positioned before the existing (more advanced) Multi-Agent Harness Engineering Handbook link as the recommended starting point for beginners.

### Changed
- **[2026-08-21]**: docs(constitution): closed 3 gaps between settled ADR decisions and `CONSTITUTION.md`'s delegated `docs/constitution/*.md` files, found via a full 39-ADR audit — ADR-0025 (Root Directory Protection / `workspace-schema.json § rootAllowlist` default-deny) added to `docs/constitution/01-folder-structure.md` §1.2; ADR-0026 (every new variant requires an ADR) and ADR-0028 (`PROMOTION_CHECKLIST.md` Universal vs. Domain-specific condition split) added to `docs/constitution/07-new-project.md` as new §7.6. The other 36 ADRs were confirmed already covered, superseded, or narrow single-script details not warranting workspace-wide policy documentation.

### Changed
- **[2026-08-21]**: docs(governance): reflected this session's Context Commonization Review + WS-09 structure-standardization work into the design/governance docs: `docs/governance/variant-contract.md` gained a "Context.md Structure Standard" section (parallel to the existing "README Standard"/WS-08 section, documenting WS-09's slot schema, exemptions, and severity policy); `docs/adr/0050-variant-script-inheritance-and-golden-reference-ssot.md` gained two Implementation Status entries (the "Scripts" promotion + the `Hybrid Scripting` nested-subsection bug it surfaced, and the WS-09 structural-standardization decision); `docs/constitution/00-ssot-architecture.md`'s SSOT flow table gained a WS-09 row (explicitly noting it enforces structure only, not content — no single SSOT file the way `docs/context.md`/`README.md` have); `skills/context-commonization-review/SKILL.md` (1.0.0 → 1.1.0) documents the nested-`###`-subsection defect as a known caveat to check for before running `promote-context-section.ts` without `--dry-run`, and cross-references WS-09 as a related-but-distinct concern (content dedup vs. structural conformance).

### Added
- **[2026-08-21]**: feat(validate-templates): new Check WS-09 — `docs/<variant>.context.md` structure conformance (`validate-templates.ts` 1.5.18 → 1.6.0). Follows a user-requested review of whether the "collaboration-family" variants' context files could be standardized: found 7 of 8 (`co-consult`, `co-design`, `co-develop`, `co-export`, `co-game`, `co-security`, `co-work`) already share the same skeleton (Stack → Agents → Skills → [Environment Setup] → Development Workflow → *Domain* Guidelines → File Organization Policy → Domain Rules), just with domain-flavored heading text ("Tool Stack" vs "Design Stack", "Consulting Guidelines" vs "Coding Guidelines"). Deliberately does NOT standardize content — only presence + relative order of the required slots, matched via an alias/regex table (`WS09_STRUCTURE_SCHEMA`) rather than a heading whitelist, so domain-specific extra headings stay allowed. `co-abap` (structurally distinct SAP/ABAP domain) is exempted outright rather than forced to conform. Starts `warningOnly` in `docs/templates/lifecycle-governance.json` (same rollout pattern WS-08 used) since `co-news` — a newer, still-diverging beta variant — doesn't yet conform. `co-deck` was found to already conform without any changes.

### Fixed
- **[2026-08-21]**: docs(templates): `promote-context-section.ts`'s section-removal regex stops at the next `#{2,3}` heading, so when the promoted section (`## Scripts`) had a nested `### Hybrid Scripting` subsection, the regex correctly removed `## Scripts` but left `### Hybrid Scripting` behind as an orphaned, parent-less duplicate in all 7 promoted variant files — and the same boundary issue meant the promoted content copied into `docs/context.md` never included it either, so the content was silently dropped from the SSOT for `co-consult`/`co-export` (the only two of the 7 with no `## Coding Guidelines` section carrying a second copy elsewhere). Caught by manually reviewing the remaining commonization candidates. Fixed: removed the 7 orphaned duplicates, added `### Hybrid Scripting` to `docs/context.md`'s "Scripts" section (completing the promotion correctly). The tool itself (`promote-context-section.ts`) is not yet fixed for this nested-subsection class of bug — flagged for follow-up, not addressed in this pass since no other pending candidate has the same nested-heading shape.

### Changed
- **[2026-08-21]**: docs(templates): promoted the "Scripts" section (2.4 → 2.5) — 7/10 variants (`co-consult`, `co-design`, `co-develop`, `co-export`, `co-game`, `co-security`, `co-work`) carried a char-for-char identical `audit`/`dev-sync`/`sync-md` script table + "Hybrid Scripting" note. Moved into `docs/context.md` via `promote-context-section.ts`; the section is removed wholesale from each variant file (the tool has no partial-section mode), which also dropped `co-export`'s 3-line variant-specific explanation ("co-export does not maintain its own script copies") — accepted as a known trade-off rather than treated as a defect in this pass. `co-abap` was excluded (its Scripts section carries SAP-specific entries, not the common table). `bun scripts/audit.ts`'s new `checkStalePromotedContent()` confirms no leftover duplicates.

### Added
- **[2026-08-21]**: test(l3-promotion): `test-l3-to-variant-promotion.ts` (1.0.2 → 1.1.0) — adds Test 5, `docs/context.md` coverage for the L3 scaffold → variant promotion path, closing the gap flagged at the start of this session (a real project, `co-news`, was missing `docs/context.md` due to two independent scaffold-time bugs that had no regression test). Asserts: (5a) the L3 fixture's `docs/context.md` survives `create-l3-scaffold.ts`; (5b) it's correctly *absent* from the promoted variant template output (`generate-variant.ts`'s `SKIP_IN_COPY` deliberately excludes it — a `templates/co-*/` template never carries its own `docs/context.md`, only `templates/common/` does); (5c) `docs/<variant>.context.md` is generated in the promoted output. Test 5b's expectation was initially written backwards and caught by running the test against the real pipeline before committing — confirms the existing "Check WS-07" intent in `validate-templates.ts` rather than contradicting it.

### Added
- **[2026-08-21]**: feat(audit): `checkStalePromotedContent()` (`audit.ts` 2.14.1 → 2.15.0) — resolves ADR-0050 Open Question #3, informed by this session's first real Context Commonization Review. After promoting the "Git / PR Workflow" section into `docs/context.md`, 4 variants (`co-abap`, `co-architect`, `co-consult`, `co-game`) were found to still carry the old duplicate section, undetected because `checkVariantContextCommonization()` only ever compares variants against each other — once fewer than 2 variants still had the leftover text, it silently stopped being flagged. The new check instead compares every `docs/<variant>.context.md` section directly against `templates/common/docs/context.md`, so a lingering post-promotion duplicate is caught regardless of how many variants still have it. WARN-only, mirrors the existing similarity-heuristic pattern. ADR-0050 Open Question #3 updated to record this resolution in place of the originally-proposed scheduled-review-cadence approach, which the real-world case showed wasn't the actual gap.

### Fixed
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(scripts): `upgrade-project.ts` (1.9.0 → 1.10.0) — two gaps found while upgrading 7 real projects in one session. **(1)** `.gitleaks.toml` moved out of the blind LOCKED-overwrite path into a new `mergeGitleaksToml()` — the old behavior silently dropped project-specific allowlist entries on every upgrade (found live: co-abap's exclusion for vendored ABAP source that trips the generic-api-key rule, plus a SAP developer-trial default-credential allowance, both deleted by the previous run and only caught because the next `git push`'s secret scan failed on 70 false-positive "leaks"). The merge diffs the project's current `regexes`/`paths` entries against the incoming template and appends anything project-only, rather than discarding it. **(2)** `SYNC_IF_NEWER: scripts/` now calls a new `reconcileScriptRegistry()` after every file copy — previously script *content* was synced by version comparison but the project's own `scripts/SCRIPTS.md` registry was never touched, so `verify-scripts.ts`/`lifecycle-sync-audit.ts` failed after every single upgrade (stale version numbers on existing rows, "Unregistered script" for newly-added files like `helpers/context-sections.ts`) and needed manual reconciliation each time — hit in all 7 of the session's real-project upgrades (6-8 mismatches each). The L0 (workspace root) registry is used as the version/row source of truth; existing project rows have only their version column replaced (every other project-specific column, e.g. differing "layer" label conventions, is preserved untouched), and missing rows are appended verbatim from L0. Verified via an isolated regex test against a real project's registry (both the update-existing-row and append-missing-row paths) plus a live re-run against `co-abap-plugin`, which also surfaced and fixed a subtler bug in the gitleaks merge: comments containing apostrophes (e.g. "AI session notes") were tripping the naive single-quote string-literal matcher into misparsing everything after them — fixed by stripping comment-only lines before extracting array entries.

### Changed
- **[2026-08-21]**: docs(templates): established an explicit "Git / PR Workflow" heading in `co-abap`, `co-deck`, and `co-news` — the 3 remaining stable variants without one (checked at the user's request after the "Git / PR Workflow" promotion covered only 6/10 variants). None duplicate the now-common content; each is a short pointer to `docs/context.md` § Git / PR Workflow plus only the genuinely variant-specific ordering rule: `co-abap` — `/sync` runs after `/transport` (CTS release), split out of the "Development Workflow" section it had been folded into; `co-news` — `/sync` runs only after the Editorial Review Gate passes; `co-deck` — no override, none of its 4 production workflows alter git/PR mechanics. `co-abap`'s "Development Workflow" section keeps its ABAP-specific triage/post-write/transport steps, now without the git/PR mechanics mixed in.
- **[2026-08-21]**: refactor(templates): `co-game`'s "Git / PR Workflow" section actually existed but wasn't in the previous promotion's 6-variant list — user asked why, which surfaced that it used `→` (arrow) instead of `—` (em dash) as its list-bullet character, a purely cosmetic difference that nonetheless dropped its line-overlap similarity to 41.7%, just under `checkVariantContextCommonization()`'s 50% threshold, so it was never flagged as a candidate in the first place. Confirmed with the user this was accidental drift, not a deliberate choice (unlike `co-security`'s commit-type convention difference, resolved separately) — removed `co-game`'s now-redundant duplicate section directly, since the content already exists in `templates/common/docs/context.md` post-promotion.
- **[2026-08-21]**: refactor(templates): first real Context Commonization Review promotion (ADR-0050 Part 3) — the "Git / PR Workflow" section, previously duplicated near-identically across `co-consult`, `co-design`, `co-develop`, `co-export`, `co-security`, and `co-work`'s `docs/<variant>.context.md`, is now a single copy in `templates/common/docs/context.md` (2.3 → 2.4). Before promoting, aligned `co-security`'s `/sync "security: description"` commit-type convention to the `/sync "feat: description"` all other variants use — `promote-context-section.ts`'s divergence check had flagged this as a real, deliberate difference (not just low text overlap) that would have been silently discarded otherwise; confirmed with the user this specific difference should be normalized rather than preserved. `co-security`'s phase-boundary-specific "Suggested commit message format" example (`docs/co-security.context.md`, a different section describing its 5-phase multi-commit pattern) was deliberately left untouched — it's legitimately domain-specific content, not shared boilerplate.

### Added
- **[2026-08-21]**: feat(governance): built the execution tooling ADR-0050 Part 3 (Context Commonization Review) needed but didn't yet have — `scripts/promote-context-section.ts` (new) extracts a duplicated section, inserts it into `docs/context.md`, bumps its version footer, and removes it from each specified variant's `docs/<variant>.context.md`, always printing a per-variant line diff against the canonical text first (a dry-run promotion of "Git / PR Workflow" across 6 variants found `co-security` used a different `/sync` commit-type convention than the other 5 — 83-100% textual overlap, but not actually interchangeable — exactly the case the diff step exists to catch). `skills/context-commonization-review/SKILL.md` (new) documents the full detect → decide → execute → propagate → verify procedure. `scripts/audit.ts`'s section-parsing (`checkVariantContextCommonization()`) extracted to shared `scripts/helpers/context-sections.ts` (2.14.0 → 2.14.1) so the detector and the new executor never independently re-implement the same heading-parsing logic. `docs/context.md` (2.2 → 2.3) and ADR-0050 updated to name the actual tooling instead of describing it only in prose.
- **[2026-08-21]**: feat(governance): `docs/context.md`'s dormant version-footer convention (`*context.md version: X.Y*`) is now wired into `upgrade-project.ts`'s `VARIANT_DOCS_SYNC` — previously registered under `DOCS_MERGE_FILES`'s managed-block merge, a silent no-op since the common template carries no managed-block markers. `VARIANT_DOCS_SYNC`'s source resolution generalized from a variant-dir-only lookup to `resolveTemplate()` (variant, then common) so it can reach `docs/context.md`'s common-only SSOT (`upgrade-project.ts` 1.8.1 → 1.9.0). Verified via dry-run against `Projects/co-news/` (`docs/context.md` v2.1 → v2.2 detected and would update; conflict detection via `isLocallyModified()` preserved for projects that hand-edited the file).
- **[2026-08-21]**: feat(governance): extended ADR-0050 (Variant Script Inheritance) with Part 3, applying the same "common is SSOT, never duplicate" principle to `docs/<variant>.context.md` — with a periodic Context Commonization Review process (trigger: after each new variant, or every 5 variants/quarterly) instead of a hard gate, since cross-variant prose duplication needs human judgment about whether it's genuinely universal (→ promote to `docs/context.md`) or only shared by a subset (→ extract to a shared skill instead of bloating the common file for variants that don't need it). Implemented `checkVariantContextCommonization()` in `scripts/audit.ts` (2.13.3 → 2.14.0, WARN-only, mirrors the existing `checkVariantScriptDrift()` line-overlap heuristic, section-scoped by heading) — a first real run across the 10 stable variants found 9 flagged section headings, several at 100% overlap across 6-7 variants (`git / pr workflow`, `scripts`, `skills`), confirming the duplication-accumulation concern that prompted this work. `docs/context.md` bumped 2.1 → 2.2 (fixed the ADR-0021 broken-link issue found while investigating, added the new Lifecycle Management subsection documenting this process). `skills/upgrade-project/SKILL.md` (1.1.0 → 1.2.0) documents both mechanisms.

### Fixed
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(docs): `templates/common/docs/context.md`'s "Platform Hooks & Governance Enforcement" section linked `[ADR-0021](../../adr/0021-platform-settings-parity-policy.md)` — a path that only resolves from the workspace root, but this file is copied verbatim into every L2/L3 project at varying depths (and later into `templates/co-*/` at Phase B), where the same relative path is wrong. Surfaced by `validate-docs-links.ts` when syncing the `docs/context.md` recovered for `Projects/co-news/` (see the entry below) — the link had been silently broken in every project carrying this file; `co-newbiz`'s manually-recovered copy had already independently rewritten around it. Replaced with unlinked prose, matching that precedent.
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(scaffold): `create-l3-scaffold.ts` (1.10.1 → 1.11.0) never copied `docs/context.md` — the immutable common project-context file — into any L3 scaffold. Root cause: `createDomainDocs()` only copied `templates/common/docs/_common/*`, and `docs/context.md` lives one level up, outside `_common/`; `new-project.ts` never had this bug because it does a full `copyDir(commonDir, projectDir)`. Found while investigating why `Projects/co-news/docs/context.md` was missing; confirmed the same defect had already hit `co-newbiz` (manually patched in #55, but the scaffold script itself was never fixed, so it kept recurring). `scripts/audit.ts` (2.13.2 → 2.13.3) also silently treated any project missing `docs/context.md` as "must be the workspace root" (`Warn`, not `Fail`) — reused the existing `isWorkspaceRoot` (`CONSTITUTION.md` accessibility) signal so a real L2/L3 project missing the file now fails audit instead of passing silently. Recovered `docs/context.md` for `Projects/co-news/` (the only project still missing it; `co-abap`, `co-abap-plugin`, `co-architect`, `co-consult`, `co-deck`, `co-game`, `co-newbiz`, `safety_os` already had it).
- **[2026-08-21]**: fix(scaffold): `copyCommonOverlay()` in `create-l3-scaffold.ts` switched from an allowlist ("copy only these named files") to `COMMON_OVERLAY_EXCLUDE` ("copy everything except these") — the same "default include, explicit exclude" pattern `new-project.ts`'s `copyDir()` already used. The allowlist is what let `docs/context.md` go missing for months; verifying the fix surfaced the same class of bug a second time — `.claude/skills.json` and `.gemini/skills.json` were also silently absent from every L3 scaffold (including `co-news`, `co-newbiz`, now retrofitted). Also added: a `docs/context.md merge=ours` `.gitattributes` protection rule at scaffold time (previously only `new-project.ts` added this; retrofitted onto `co-news`/`co-newbiz`), and an immediate post-scaffold `bun scripts/audit.ts` run (previously only `new-project.ts` self-verified at creation time — `create-l3-scaffold.ts` left problems to surface only at the first `/sync`). Updated `--dry-run` preview output and `skills/create-variant/SKILL.md` to match, and fixed a stale `new-project.sh`/`.ps1` reference in the latter (ADR-0036 removed those files).

### Added
- **[2026-08-20]**: `docs/designs/2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design_ko.md` §19 — Investment Vehicle Structuring addendum. User-flagged gap during Stage 5's system pilot re-run (`Projects/co-newbiz/docs/pilots/pilot-001-system-rerun.md`): the design's "deal structure hypothesis" (Pre-FS deliverable) covers acquisition mechanics (stake percentage, spin-off vs. license for R&BD) but never the investment vehicle question — what legal entity holds the deal (direct corporate balance sheet vs. a newly formed SPV vs. routing through a holding-company subsidiary). Decision: the deal structure hypothesis gains four mandatory review axes (tax structure, liability isolation, financing leverage, exit structure), applying to both Brown Field and Green Field, owned across `brownfield-agent`/`valuation-agent`/`legal-counsel`/`risk-officer`. Implementation (a new skill, a co-newbiz-repo ADR, and the procedure-schema extension) is deferred to `Projects/co-newbiz/` as follow-on work — this addendum records the design decision itself.
- **[2026-08-20]**: docs(design): co-newbiz Stage 2-4 progress and scope-expansion addendum (`docs/designs/2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design_ko.md` §18, new). Records two decisions made in the co-newbiz repository that affect this design document directly: (1) O-3 (real deal for the Stage 3 manual pilot) resolved not by waiting on an organizational answer but by researching real, publicly documented deals as the pilot basis — synthetic cases remain disallowed per the original design principle, but a real precedent satisfies the same "does this data exist in reality" test a fabricated case cannot. Eleven real cases across ten industries were walked by 2026-08-20, exercising all four possible Brown Field antitrust outcomes (unconditional approval, behavioral remedy, structural remedy, prohibition) with zero procedure logic errors found — every finding was a real-case-surfaced scope gap, applied immediately to the relevant procedure or checklist. (2) R&BD (Research & Business Development — technology/IP acquisition, license-in, internal-venture spin-off) added as a Brown Field sub-type (`case_subtype: rbd`) rather than a third top-level review type alongside Green/Brown Field, since its deal mechanics are BF-shaped (equity/license structures), not GF-shaped (site selection) — grounded immediately in a real case, POSCO's internal-venture program spinning off Amberoad (2023), with every new checklist item and procedure extension validating cleanly on first use. Full pilot records and procedure changes live in the co-newbiz repository (`Projects/co-newbiz/docs/pilots/`, `Projects/co-newbiz/procedures/`, `Projects/co-newbiz/checklists/`), untracked here per `.gitignore` `/*/`.
- **[2026-08-20]**: fix(scripts): repair two L0-scaffold defects hit while syncing co-newbiz Stage 2. **(1)** `test-platform-parity.ts` was misclassified `L0+L1` in `scripts/SCRIPTS.md` despite being an L0-only tool (it tests L0 → L1/L2 template propagation, meaningless standalone in an L3 project with no `templates/` directory) — reclassified to `L0`, pruned the stale `templates/common/scripts/test-platform-parity.ts` copy via `propagate-to-templates.ts --prune --apply`, and added `audit:test-platform-parity` to `lifecycle-sync-audit.ts`'s `INTENTIONAL_CROSS_REFS` allowlist (Check X) since the reference in `templates/common/scripts/audit.ts` is `existsSync`-guarded, same pattern as the existing `pre-commit:validate-templates` entry. **(2)** `readme-lifecycle-audit.ts` (1.0.3 → 1.0.4) computed `isWorkspaceRoot` from `relPath === 'README.md'` alone, so any L3 project's own root README.md — not just the actual workspace root — was held to the 6 workspace-only required sections (What Is This?, Repository Structure, Session Start Checklist, Multi-Agent Workflow, License, plus Quick Start) at `error` level; gated on the existing `IS_WORKSPACE_ROOT` (`CONSTITUTION.md` presence) flag as well, so L3 projects fall through to `REQUIRED_SECTIONS_PROJECT` (empty by design) as intended. Both scripts propagated to `templates/common/` and `templates/common/scripts/SCRIPTS.md` version rows aligned (`lifecycle-sync-audit.ts` 1.4.5 → 1.4.6 for the allowlist addition). `bun scripts/audit.ts` passes clean. Found and worked around locally in `Projects/co-newbiz/` first (untracked here); fixed at source per the workspace's established scaffold-defect pattern (see the 2026-08-19 five-defect entry below).
- **[2026-08-20]**: docs(design): co-newbiz Stage 1 addendum (`docs/designs/2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design_ko.md` §17, new). All 12 Stage 1 planning meetings (M0 through M8, including M7-1/M7-2/M7-3) ran in the co-newbiz repository against the real 34-agent roster — the design's three prior review meetings predate the roster and used inline role-play instead. Key outcomes folded into the design: O-2 resolved (shipbuilding-adjacent, KR+EU); RAG thresholds fixed with real IRR/DSCR numbers and confirmed as a priority-ordered rule rather than a weighted average; a correction to the chain-grading model (grade is driven by the number of *statistical* hops, not total hop count — a chain with one engineering hop and one statistical hop can be Grade B, since engineering hops don't degrade the grade); M&A special-topic findings named to concrete `assumption_key`s with an explicit evaluation order (certification transferability resolves last, since it can strip the entire acquisition premium); greenfield site scoring redesigned as a hard pre-filter followed by a weighted score, rather than folding infrastructure minimums into the weights; information protection made fully operational (concrete cohort-size minimum, a static repository-layer need-to-know contract, a four-step logged MNPI workflow); and a correction to a design default — the concurrent-deal cap starts at 1, not the previously stated 3, since that number assumed a stronger reviewer baseline than is actually confirmed. No conflicts surfaced across the 12 meetings at the integrated review; Stage 1 is formally closed with Stage 2 entry criteria met. Two items remain escalated to the user rather than defaulted: O-3 (a real deal for the Stage 3 manual pilot) and O-16 (reviewer allocation), the latter now confirmed to affect the concurrent-deal cap as well as the schedule estimate. Meeting transcripts live in the co-newbiz repository (`Projects/co-newbiz/memory/meeting-2026-08-20-*.md`), which is untracked here per `.gitignore` `/*/`.
- **[2026-08-19]**: fix(scaffold): repair five defects that left every newly scaffolded variant broken — `scripts/create-l3-scaffold.ts` (1.9.2 → 1.10.0), `scripts/sync-md.ts` (1.2.0 → 1.3.0), and `templates/common/.github/workflows/ci.yml`. Found while scaffolding `Projects/co-newbiz/`; each would have hit every future variant, so they are fixed at source rather than worked around per project. **(1) No `package.json` was ever produced.** `bunInstall()` checked `scripts/package.json` — a path no template has ever populated — and the overlay never copied `templates/common/package.json` either, so install was silently skipped. This stayed hidden because Bun resolves imports by walking up to the parent workspace's `node_modules`: scaffolds under `Projects/` appear to work, but each is its own git repo, so a standalone clone would have neither manifest nor dependencies. The manifest is now adapted rather than copied — variant-specific name and description, `private: true`, the `workspace-scripts` L0 marker dropped, and script entries pruned when their target `.ts` is absent, since Tier 3 bootstrap scripts are excluded from L3 copies. **(2) The `co-` prefix was applied unconditionally**, so an already-prefixed name yielded `co-co-newbiz` in `variant.json`, the AGENTS.md header, the promotion path, and the `templates/co-*` collision check; normalized through one `toVariantSlug()` helper. **(3) `memory/MEMORY.md` was advertised by the dry run but never written** — not cosmetic, because its absence triggered defect 5's sibling in `sync-md.ts`. **(4) A dead `setup.sh not found` warning** fired on every scaffold for a file that exists nowhere in `templates/`; the code path is removed. **(5) A fresh scaffold failed its own `audit.ts`** — the generated `agents/pm.md` lacked the `lifecycle` frontmatter `validate-agents.ts` requires, and since audit is a FATAL gate in `/sync`, every new variant was blocked from its first commit until someone hand-patched the stub. Added the frontmatter plus the paired `docs/lifecycle/agents/pm.md` record, since a lifecycle block pointing at a nonexistent record is worse than neither. Separately, **`sync-md.ts` was non-idempotent**: it keyed its whole migration off a single `## Sessions` guard and then appended the Meetings and ADRs sections unconditionally, while its heading regex demanded exactly `# Memory Index` — so a suffixed title like `# Memory Index — co-newbiz` left the guard permanently false and re-appended both sections on every run (three copies each after two syncs). Each section is now inserted only when absent and the heading match tolerates a suffix. Finally, **the common CI template pinned `setup-bun` to a commit that does not resolve**, failing Documentation Audit at action-resolution time on every scaffolded project — the workspace's own workflows use a valid pin, so only the template had drifted; `bun install` ran in a directory with no manifest behind a `continue-on-error: true` that masked it; and `gitleaks-action` cannot work on private repos without `pull-requests: read` plus a licence, so the job now runs the gitleaks container directly, which is all a pass/fail scan needs. Verified by scaffolding a throwaway variant that passes `audit.ts` with 0 errors and 0 warnings out of the box, and by running `sync-md.ts` three times against a suffixed heading with section counts holding at 1. Survey of existing projects: `co-architect`, `co-news`, and `safety_os` also lack `package.json` — reported only, since they are separate git repositories.
- **[2026-08-19]**: docs(design): **co-newbiz design finalized at v1.0** (`docs/designs/2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design_ko.md` Status Approved → Final; spec `2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design-ko` draft → approved). Added a §0 confirmation summary and the design-revision history (draft → 1st meeting finding omissions → 2nd finding excess and contradiction → certification/axis-C revision → 3rd resolving the open-issue backlog → three-stage review → final). A numeric consistency pass ran before declaring it final rather than trusting totals carried through four rounds of revision, and it caught two real errors: functional-domain agents were declared 19 then 20 while the enumerated rows are **21** (the original 19 was already off by one, before `certification-agent` existed), correcting the roster to **34**; and skill totals were stale at "93 → 88 / new 72" while enumerating the group tables gives **86 new + 16 reused = 102**. Every group's declared count matched its actual rows — only the prose roll-ups were wrong, so the merge note no longer carries a global total that will drift again. Final shape: 34 agents across four domains, 102 skills, three team axes (governance / system / deal review), document-first execution in six stages, three-stage review (AI formal → AI cross-domain → human final), ADRs 0001–0067, estimated ~230–250 AI sessions and ~15–18 human review-days at ±50%. Two inputs remain outstanding before work starts: reviewer allocation of ≥2.5 days/week (O-16, Stage 0 entry condition) and a real deal for the manual pilot (O-3, before Stage 3).
- **[2026-08-19]**: docs(design): **co-newbiz** adopts a three-stage review — AI 1st/2nd, human final (`docs/designs/2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design_ko.md` §10.1-2, new). Human review was the identified bottleneck at ~37 days; moving formal and cross-domain review to AI cuts that to ~15–18 days (AI sessions rise ~190 → ~230–250), improving calendar to **2–3 months with a dedicated reviewer and 4–5 with a part-time one**. Savings are estimated per artifact type rather than as one blanket figure: repetitive output (88 skills, 33 agents, checklists) saves 60–70%, code and schema 40–50% since automated checks already exist, and judgment-bearing output (regulation book, ADRs, security model) only 20–30%. No new 2nd-review machinery was built — §5.7's Phase 1.5 cross-validation matrix already does that job and was elevated to a formal stage, with same-domain review counting only as 1st stage. The central risk is that **an AI reviewing AI output shares its blind spots**, so correlated errors pass silently; four mitigations apply — author ≠ reviewer enforced across domains, deterministic checks (lint/schema/tests) run before any inference-based review, humans sample-audit AI-passed items to measure reviewer agreement (a governance KPI that auto-raises human review share when it drops), and **silence is never read as approval**: the human screen shows no "PASS" stamp but splits output into verified / could-not-verify / needs-judgment, with `review_stage.unverified_items_json` NOT NULL so an AI reviewer must explicitly declare what it could not check. Approval authority is unchanged — regulations, gate approvals, investment decisions, the privacy/security model, and ADRs still cannot pass on AI review alone; pre-review shortens reading time, not accountability. Human rejections of AI-passed items feed back into review-rule improvement. O-16 relaxed from "one dedicated reviewer" to "2.5 days/week or more" — still a Stage 0 entry condition but no longer a stop condition. ADRs 0064–0067; new open issues O-18 (reliability threshold and sampling rate) and O-19 (whether to run AI review on a different model family).
- **[2026-08-19]**: docs(design): third **co-newbiz** meeting resolves the open-issue backlog and finally produces a schedule estimate (`docs/designs/2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design_ko.md` §10.1-1, §15 rewritten). O-7 had been deferred three times while blocking O-9 and O-14; it became tractable only after splitting the estimation unit in two — AI sessions and human review-days — and recognizing that **human review is the bottleneck, not AI execution** (88 skills generate quickly; someone still has to read and approve them). Result: ~190 AI sessions / ~37 human review-days + 3 weeks of pilot calendar, giving 3–4 months with a dedicated reviewer plus 0.3 FTE domain review, or 7–9 months with only a part-time reviewer, stated at ±50% since no measured baseline exists. That reframing then unblocked three more: Thin Slice and the manual pilot may run **in parallel** (different resource pools — AI execution vs. human analyst — and cross-checking each other's numbers is a bonus); axis-C load is ~0.25–0.3 reviewer FTE per concurrent deal; and the concurrent-deal cap is expressed as a formula, `min(reviewer capacity / 0.3, wall_group-compatible assignments)`, because information isolation often binds before headcount does — three reviewers each walled to a different deal is not capacity of three. Also **verified that the sequential-branch constraint the design repeated throughout is stale**: `.gitattributes:34-38` carries `merge=union` for the shared pipeline files (landed 2026-08-18) and `templates/common/.gitattributes:32-35` has it too, so scaffolded projects inherit it — Wave parallelization is available and "1 PR per wave" is now a review-granularity preference, not a technical limit. Reviewer availability escalated from schedule risk to **Stage 0 entry condition** (O-16, new critical path). Organization-dependent issues converted from "waiting on an answer" to working default + decision deadline; O-3 (a real deal for the pilot) is the sole exception, since a synthetic case catches broken process steps but never catches "this data does not exist in reality". O-8 timing corrected from M8 to end of Stage 2, because judging whether the three thin agents can be absorbed requires the procedures that define gate participation. ADRs 0060–0063. Transcript: `memory/meeting-2026-08-19-co-newbiz-open-issues.md`.
- **[2026-08-19]**: docs(design): **co-newbiz** gains certification/regulatory response and promotes deal analysis to a third team axis (`docs/designs/2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design_ko.md`). **(1) Certification (§8.8-1, new)** — kept strictly separate from permits, since a permit is a government licence to *operate* while a certification is a third-party attestation that lets you *sell*; different issuers, procedures, and critical paths, and conflating them loses both. Covers scheme identification (class society type approval, IATF/API/ASME/PED/ATEX, CE/UL/KC, RoHS/REACH), gap analysis, and — the part usually missing — a four-way comparison of how to *obtain* it (in-house, acquire a certified company, partnership, OEM). `certification.blocks_revenue` forces modelled revenue to zero until mandatory certs land, structurally preventing optimistic ramp-up curves, and certification transferability on change of control becomes a mandatory M&A cross-check (5 → 6 validators): "the target already holds it" is a standard valuation-premium argument, but several schemes require re-assessment on legal-entity change, in which case the premium vanishes. **(2) Deal analysis as axis C (§2, §8.9-1 new)** — reverses the design's founding premise ("this team does not analyze deals") and supersedes the second meeting's methodology-only resolution. Justified as more than added scope: running real deals through the team's own procedures exposes procedural defects immediately, so axis C is the quality-assurance loop for axes A and B, with defects surfacing as `interpretation`/`waiver` records that drive amendment priority. Two boundaries are fixed and marked non-negotiable — agents inherit the calling user's permissions and get **no accounts of their own** (a dedicated agent service account is the easiest way to defeat the need-to-know model, so its absence is a verification item), and agents produce analysis plus a *proposed* RAG grade while **gate approval and the investment decision stay with humans** (`analysis_task.reviewed_by` NULL blocks the gate). All agent output is stamped with agent/model/version/requester/reviewer. Roster 32 → 33 (`certification-agent`), skills +7, ADRs 0053–0059, open issues O-12–O-15. Also revises the second meeting's R10: agent tier now splits by work type rather than phase, since misjudging valuation or certification transferability costs the same after launch as during design.
- **[2026-08-19]**: docs(design): second design-refinement meeting for **co-newbiz** — 10 revisions applied to `docs/designs/2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design_ko.md`. Where the first meeting hunted for omissions, this one hunted for what was present but wrong or excessive, with rule authors asked to argue against their own rules. Two first-round decisions were reversed as over-strict: the RAG data-confidence cap would have pinned nearly every project at Amber (the exact "nobody reads the signal" failure the design warns about) — kept, but now paired with a Stage 3 calibration dry-run and an actionable confidence dimension that states what evidence would lift the grade; and blanket MNPI exclusion from MCP would have blocked the system's highest-value use case — replaced with per-session insider enrollment (scoped token + `insider_list` membership + access logging + automatic roster update), since the regulation demands traceability, not prohibition. Sharpest structural fix: `procedures/*.yaml` and `skills/*.md` both described workflows with no stated authority, so they are now split by axis — procedures own WHAT/WHEN (machine-executed, versioned, `project_policy_pin`-scoped), skills own HOW (technique, judgment), with a lint rejecting skills that restate gate steps. Also adds Stage 2.5 Thin Vertical Slice (DB core + one proxy chain + NPV + markdown report; no UI/auth/MCP) so the design's central "data changes → report regenerates" claim is proven early rather than late in Stage 5; documents the dual role of deal-analysis agents (methodology during build, on-demand advisors after go-live) to resolve an internal contradiction; introduces a skill-existence bar (distinct trigger + owner + non-trivial artifact, 93 → 88 skills); defines the `project_policy_pin` × Group IC interaction; requires `source`/`verified_on`/`maintainer` on every region-profile field; and adds phase-dependent agent tiering. Transcript: `memory/meeting-2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design-refinement.md`.
- **[2026-08-19]**: docs(design): add `docs/designs/2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design_ko.md` — full design for **co-newbiz**, a new-business-review AI team and system. The team owns two co-equal axes: (A) governance artifacts — policies, procedures, guidelines, checklists — including their ongoing operation (compliance monitoring, waivers, interpretation registry, KPIs), and (B) implementing that governance as a system and maintaining it (DB, API, UI, MCP, deployment, ops, security). Roster is 32 agents across four domains — **functional** (19, governance + review methodology), **technical** (9, system build/run), **industry** (1, profile-driven), **region** (1, profile-driven) — with PM coordinating the four domain leads under the workspace's flat PM Gateway. Reuses `Projects/safety_os` structural patterns (`agents/{_core,_shared,domains}`, `workflows/_shared/REFERENCE-SPEC.md` reference-override model, `legal-glossary.yaml` tooling-consumed glossary), `templates/co-consult`'s DART→IFRS→KPI→ROIC-driver-tree financial pipeline, `Projects/co-architect/mcp/` server pattern, and `scripts/lib/{auth,ssrf}.ts`. Execution is document-first across 6 stages (agents → facilitated meetings → regulation book + manuals → detailed process + **manual pilot** → SRS + traceability matrix → build), with a 3-tier MVP boundary in which information protection is the one non-descopable item. Registered as spec `2026-08-19-co-newbiz-design-ko`; plan-review meeting transcript at `memory/meeting-2026-08-19-co-newbiz-plan-review.md` (8 gaps found, 2 rated critical: personal-data handling and project-level need-to-know access control). Implementation lands in `Projects/co-newbiz/` (untracked here per `.gitignore` `/*/`).
- **[2026-08-19]**: revert(ci): restore `.github/dependabot.yml` — the earlier decommission was based on a misunderstanding; only the Actions *permissions* matter (runner allocation issue) is out of scope, not GitHub Actions as a whole. Workflows re-enabled and branch-protection required status checks restored to `test (ubuntu/macos/windows, 18.x)`.
- **[2026-08-19]**: chore(ci): decommission GitHub Actions per maintainer decision — CI/validation runs locally via the `/sync` pipeline gates (audit.ts, gitleaks, integration tests) instead. Disabled the Test Suite and Weekly Health Check workflows (`disabled_manually`), removed `.github/dependabot.yml` (stops Dependabot PRs; the Dependabot Updates workflow is GitHub-managed and cannot be disabled via API, but has no update sources without the config), and dropped the branch-protection required status checks (`test (ubuntu/macos/windows, 18.x)`) so PRs merge normally without CI. The stuck queued run (32207250100, no runner allocated) cannot be deleted until it leaves the queue and will age out automatically. Workflow files are retained for potential future re-enable.
- **[2026-08-19]**: fix(ci): `test.yml` had not executed since 2026-08-16 despite active pushes to main (workflow state "active", triggers configured for `push`/`pull_request` on main and `pr/**` — zero runs for 5 merged PRs). A manual dispatch attempt failed with HTTP 422 because the workflow lacks a `workflow_dispatch` trigger, leaving no way to verify or force-run the suite. Added `workflow_dispatch:` to the trigger list so the Test Suite can be run on demand as a stopgap while the silent non-triggering (likely a repository-level Actions event delivery issue) is investigated via GitHub support/repo settings.

- **[2026-08-19]**: fix(security): deferred Low-tier findings from the PM-led security review, all closed. `new-project.ts` project-name regex no longer allows `/` (flat names under workspace root only); `analyze-git-history.ts` clamps `-n` to 1..10000 (root + common mirrors); `dev-sync.ts` commit messages collapse newlines/control chars before `git -m`/`gh --title` (root + common mirrors); pre-commit regex fallback adds GitHub OAuth (`gho_`), fine-grained PAT (`github_pat_`), and Slack (`xox*`) token patterns; pre-push fallback globs extended to js/mjs/py/sh/toml/cfg/ini/html to match pre-commit coverage; co-deck `generate-themes-manifest.test.ts` converted `execSync` → `execFileSync` argv arrays (last non-argv subprocess call in templates); co-abap `vsp-publish.ts` sanitizes commit messages to a single line. - **[2026-08-19]**: fix(security): PM-led security review remediation (project-review skill, 4 parallel agents + cross-validation) — 1 Critical, 5 High, 8 Moderate findings fixed in one pass. **C-1**: `create-l3-scaffold.ts` `overlayItems` now copies `.gitleaks.toml` and `.github` into L3 scaffolds (previously scaffolded prototypes had no secret scanning or CI; `new-project.ts` was already covered via full common copy). **H**: removed `continue-on-error` from weekly health check's gitleaks and dependency-audit steps (repo is public, gitleaks-action needs no license — failures now fail loudly); added `github-actions` ecosystem to `.github/dependabot.yml` (pinned action SHAs now auto-update); `audit.ts` Windows device-name deletion PowerShell fallback replaced with shell-free `fs.rmSync` (removes quote-injection path, root+common mirrors); `templates/co-security/.gitleaks.toml` provisioned (docs claimed enforcement, no config existed); `audit.ts` 2.13.2 adds workflow permission hygiene check (fails on `write-all`/`all` grants, warns on missing `permissions:` block). **M**: 18 stale gitleaks path exclusions removed (nonexistent dirs, rescan clean); `team-builder.ts` `run()` refactored to argv-array signature (no more `split(" ")`); slug validation added to `setup-github-branch-protection.ts`; `validate-pm-extends.ts` `execSync`→`execFileSync` (last shell call in scripts/); `remove-project.ts` adds workspace-boundary + project-marker guard before recursive delete; root `.gitignore` copied to all 10 variants; `@types/js-yaml` moved dependencies→devDependencies; commented example actions in common ci.yml annotated with pin guidance.

- **[2026-08-18]**: fix(co-deck): `templates/co-deck/scripts/co-deck/handbook/deploy-handbook.ts` still used an `execSync`-based `run()` helper with shell command strings for `gh auth status`, the secret-scan grep, `git rev-parse`, and `git push` — the same shell-invocation pattern eliminated from both Handbooks repos during their project-review fixes. Converted all 4 call sites to the script's existing `runArgs()` helper (`execFileSync` argument arrays, injection-resistant regardless of input content) and removed the now-unused `run()`/`execSync` import; the secret-scan error-message matcher was updated for the new error prefix. Verified: `bun build` clean, `grep execSync` → 0 matches, `bun test scripts/co-deck/tests/deploy-readme-patch.test.ts` → 8 pass.

- **[2026-08-18]**: fix(templates): `templates/co-abap/scripts/co-abap/dispatch-parallel.ts` and `dispatch-serial.ts` (ADR-0050 variant wrappers) imported the common dispatchers via `../../dispatch-*.ts` — from their `scripts/co-abap/` location this resolves to the *project root*, one level above the common copies at `scripts/`, so the wrappers were unimportable in every scaffolded co-abap project (verified: `bun scripts/co-abap/dispatch-parallel.ts --help` fails with "Cannot find module" in `Projects/co-abap`, which carries the same broken import). Corrected to `../dispatch-*.ts`. Found while aligning `Projects/co-abap-plugin` with the template — that project's copies and `Projects/co-abap`'s copies were fixed in the same pass.


- **[2026-08-18]**: fix(git): eliminate recurring CHANGELOG.md / memory/*.md merge conflicts by adding git's built-in `merge=union` union merge driver to `.gitattributes` for the append-only pipeline files (`CHANGELOG.md`, `templates/CHANGELOG.md`, `memory/*.md`, `docs/VERSION_MANIFEST.md`, `scripts/README.md`). These files are touched on every `/sync` commit (CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` entries, daily session summaries, regenerated manifests), so parallel PR branches always edit the same anchor lines and the second merge always conflicted — the sequential-branch process rule (docs/designs/sequential-pr-merge-policy-design.md) was proven insufficient. Union merge auto-combines both sides' lines instead of raising conflict markers; verified by simulation (both branches' entries preserved, zero conflict markers). Transient duplicate rows in fully-regenerated files are overwritten at the next sync. Propagated to `templates/common/.gitattributes` (inherited by all new projects), `templates/co-deck/.gitattributes` (variant overlay), and all 8 live `Projects/*/.gitattributes`. Design: `docs/designs/2026-08-18-changelog-memory-conflict-fix-design.md`.

Also cleaned up several untracked/gitignored local artifact directories that had accumulated (`.gateguard-state/`, `.playwright-mcp/` stale browser snapshots, `.tmp_review/` handbook review extracts, `scratch/` design tokens, `.claude/worktrees/variant-parity-sync` empty worktree) — no tracked files affected besides the log removal.
